People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess the main thing is I’m just… worn out all the time. Like, even if I’ve been in bed all night, I wake up and my whole body feels heavy, like sandbags or something. Getting through a workday takes everything I have, and then even folding laundry feels like this huge project.

I mean, I do market research, so I’m used to long days staring at numbers and reports, and sometimes I tell myself everyone’s tired, you know? My coworker was joking last week about needing three coffees just to answer emails, and I laughed, but it’s not really funny when I get home and the laundry basket is just sitting there like it’s judging me. So… yeah, that’s why I came in. I’m tired in a way that doesn’t feel normal.

Yeah… I think so. Most days I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling in my chest, like before anything has even happened the day already feels… kind of pointless.

Yeah, that’s changed. I used to be pretty good about making time for things—hiking on the weekends, yoga, messing around in my garden. I used to take pictures of birds too, which sounds kind of nerdy, I know, but I really liked it.

I don’t know… anxious, exactly? I mean, I worry about normal things, I guess—work stuff, whether I forgot to send some report, whether the house is a mess. But then I also feel like everyone worries about that.

It’s funny, my neighbor has this dog that barks at every delivery truck, and sometimes I’ll be sitting there hearing it and suddenly I’m thinking about how I need to order paper towels, and then I’m thinking about money, and then work, and then I kind of lose track of what I was doing. But I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just… my brain being annoying.

So, I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it as the main thing.

Getting through a workday takes everything I have, and then even folding laundry feels like this huge project.\n\nI mean, I do market research, so I’m used to long days staring at numbers and reports, and sometimes I tell myself everyone’s tired, you know? My coworker was joking last week about needing three coffees just to answer emails, and I laughed, but it’s not really funny when I get home and the laundry basket is just sitting there like it’s judging me. So… yeah, that’s why I came in. I’m tired in a way that doesn’t feel normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling in my chest, like before anything has even happened the day already feels… kind of pointless.\n\nSometimes I’ll sit at the kitchen table with my coffee and just stare out at the yard. There’s this one patch where the grass never grows right, and I start thinking I should fix it, then somehow I’m thinking about the whole house and work and everything, and it just feels like there isn’t really a good version of the future coming. I used to take pictures of birds too, which sounds kind of nerdy, I know, but I really liked it.\n\nLately I just… don’t. I’ll tell myself I should go for a walk or pull weeds or get my camera out, and then it feels like too much effort for something I’m not even sure I’ll enjoy. I’ve canceled plans to hike more than once, and my camera’s just sitting in the closet. It’s weird, because those used to be the things that made me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, exactly? But then I also feel like everyone worries about that.\n\nIt’s funny, my neighbor has this dog that barks at every delivery truck, and sometimes I’ll be sitting there hearing it and suddenly I’m thinking about how I need to order paper towels, and then I’m thinking about money, and then work, and then I kind of lose track of what I was doing. But I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just… my brain being annoying.\n\nSo, I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it as the main thing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been about two months? Around when we took on the mortgage. Yeah… my husband actually mentioned that. He said I’ve been moving around the house like I’m underwater, which was kind of a weird way to put it, but I knew what he meant.

It’s funny, because everyone tells you buying a place is supposed to feel exciting, and I was doing this whole intense push around business ideas and numbers and plans, like I could just force everything to work out.\n\nAnd then after that, I don’t know… it sort of dropped out from under me. Since then it’s been this low stretch. So, yeah, roughly two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I keep thinking maybe if I just get myself out there I’ll enjoy it once I’m doing it, like with hiking or yoga or even the garden, but then I end up canceling or just… not going.\n\nI used to really look forward to taking bird photos too. I had this whole thing where I’d notice different ones around the neighborhood—there was this one little finch that kept coming back near the fence—but now the camera’s just sitting in the closet. I mean, my jeans feel… basically the same, I think. I haven’t really been weighing myself.\n\nWe have this scale in the bathroom, but it’s kind of wedged next to the laundry basket, and half the time there’s towels on it because my husband just puts things anywhere, which drives me crazy—sorry, that’s not really the question.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed any big weight change. Sorry—that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed it enough to wonder about it, but I couldn’t give you a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve made some plan or anything. And then I try to push it away, like, “That’s dramatic, stop,” but it keeps coming back.\n\nIt scares me that I’m even thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my husband actually mentioned that. Or he’ll ask me something simple—like where I put the mail, and the mail pile is its own whole disaster, honestly—and I’ll just… sit there for a second. The words are in my head, sort of, but they come out really slowly.\n\nSo, yeah, more slowed down than restless, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or blaming yourself more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Getting through a workday takes everything I have, and then even folding laundry feels like this huge project.\n\nI mean, I do market research, so I’m used to long days staring at numbers and reports, and sometimes I tell myself everyone’s tired, you know? My coworker was joking last week about needing three coffees just to answer emails, and I laughed, but it’s not really funny when I get home and the laundry basket is just sitting there like it’s judging me. So… yeah, that’s why I came in. I’m tired in a way that doesn’t feel normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling in my chest, like before anything has even happened the day already feels… kind of pointless.\n\nSometimes I’ll sit at the kitchen table with my coffee and just stare out at the yard. There’s this one patch where the grass never grows right, and I start thinking I should fix it, then somehow I’m thinking about the whole house and work and everything, and it just feels like there isn’t really a good version of the future coming. So… yes, sad, or down, or whatever you’d call that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s changed. I used to be pretty good about making time for things—hiking on the weekends, yoga, messing around in my garden. I used to take pictures of birds too, which sounds kind of nerdy, I know, but I really liked it.\n\nLately I just… don’t. I’ll tell myself I should go for a walk or pull weeds or get my camera out, and then it feels like too much effort for something I’m not even sure I’ll enjoy. I’ve canceled plans to hike more than once, and my camera’s just sitting in the closet. It’s weird, because those used to be the things that made me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, exactly? I mean, I worry about normal things, I guess—work stuff, whether I forgot to send some report, whether the house is a mess. But then I also feel like everyone worries about that.\n\nIt’s funny, my neighbor has this dog that barks at every delivery truck, and sometimes I’ll be sitting there hearing it and suddenly I’m thinking about how I need to order paper towels, and then I’m thinking about money, and then work, and then I kind of lose track of what I was doing. But I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just… my brain being annoying.\n\nSo, I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it as the main thing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been about two months? Around when we took on the mortgage. It’s funny, because everyone tells you buying a place is supposed to feel exciting, and I was doing this whole intense push around business ideas and numbers and plans, like I could just force everything to work out.\n\nAnd then after that, I don’t know… it sort of dropped out from under me. Since then it’s been this low stretch. So, yeah, roughly two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I keep thinking maybe if I just get myself out there I’ll enjoy it once I’m doing it, like with hiking or yoga or even the garden, but then I end up canceling or just… not going.\n\nI used to really look forward to taking bird photos too. I had this whole thing where I’d notice different ones around the neighborhood—there was this one little finch that kept coming back near the fence—but now the camera’s just sitting in the closet. It all feels like more effort than it’s worth, which is sad because those were the things I used to actually make time for.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, my jeans feel… basically the same, I think. I haven’t really been weighing myself.\n\nWe have this scale in the bathroom, but it’s kind of wedged next to the laundry basket, and half the time there’s towels on it because my husband just puts things anywhere, which drives me crazy—sorry, that’s not really the question.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed any big weight change. Not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping or noticing any changes in your sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t been keeping track of it, really. I mean, I go to bed, and then sometimes I’m just sort of aware of the time, but I don’t know if that’s “trouble sleeping” or just normal stress stuff.\n\nOur bedroom is kind of annoying lately too because the curtains don’t block the streetlight well, and I keep meaning to order different ones, but then I get stuck comparing reviews for like an hour and don’t buy anything. Sorry—that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed it enough to wonder about it, but I couldn’t give you a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve made some plan or anything. But late at night, when my husband’s asleep and the house is quiet—except the fridge makes this clicking sound sometimes, which drives me nuts—I’ll catch myself thinking maybe he’d be better off without me. And then I try to push it away, like, “That’s dramatic, stop,” but it keeps coming back.\n\nIt scares me that I’m even thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my husband actually mentioned that. He said I’ve been moving around the house like I’m underwater, which was kind of a weird way to put it, but I knew what he meant.\n\nLike I’ll be going from the kitchen to the living room and it takes me forever, and I don’t even notice until he says something. Or he’ll ask me something simple—like where I put the mail, and the mail pile is its own whole disaster, honestly—and I’ll just… sit there for a second. The words are in my head, sort of, but they come out really slowly.\n\nSo, yeah, more slowed down than restless, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or blaming yourself more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep going back to this mortgage decision we made, and I just… I can’t stop thinking I was selfish and reckless.\n\nLike, my husband keeps reminding me that we both agreed to it, we talked it through together, and it wasn’t just me signing something in secret or whatever. But in my head it turns into, “You put the whole household at risk.” And then I’ll start thinking about interest rates, and our kitchen needs work too, which is a whole other thing because the cabinet door has been loose for months and I keep saying I’ll fix it—\n\nSorry. But yes. I’m blaming myself for it a lot more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or like you can't sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Not so much like I can’t sit still… it’s more the opposite, I think. My husband keeps saying I move around the house like I’m underwater, which sounds funny but also kind of awful because I know what he means.\n\nAnd if he asks me something simple, I’ll just… pause. Like the answer is there, but it takes forever to come out. Even my words feel slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. I mean, I’ll shift around sometimes, but that’s probably normal—our couch has this one cushion that kind of sinks in, and my husband keeps saying we should replace it—\n\nBut the thing he’s been pointing out is more that I’m slow. Since then it’s been this low stretch. So, yeah, roughly two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I keep thinking maybe if I just get myself out there I’ll enjoy it once I’m doing it, like with hiking or yoga or even the garden, but then I end up canceling or just… not going.\n\nI used to really look forward to taking bird photos too. I had this whole thing where I’d notice different ones around the neighborhood—there was this one little finch that kept coming back near the fence—but now the camera’s just sitting in the closet. It all feels like more effort than it’s worth, which is sad because those were the things I used to actually make time for.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, my jeans feel… basically the same, I think. I haven’t really been weighing myself.\n\nWe have this scale in the bathroom, but it’s kind of wedged next to the laundry basket, and half the time there’s towels on it because my husband just puts things anywhere, which drives me crazy—sorry, that’s not really the question.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed any big weight change. Not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping or noticing any changes in your sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t been keeping track of it, really. I mean, I go to bed, and then sometimes I’m just sort of aware of the time, but I don’t know if that’s “trouble sleeping” or just normal stress stuff.\n\nOur bedroom is kind of annoying lately too because the curtains don’t block the streetlight well, and I keep meaning to order different ones, but then I get stuck comparing reviews for like an hour and don’t buy anything. Sorry—that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed it enough to wonder about it, but I couldn’t give you a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve made some plan or anything. But late at night, when my husband’s asleep and the house is quiet—except the fridge makes this clicking sound sometimes, which drives me nuts—I’ll catch myself thinking maybe he’d be better off without me. And then I try to push it away, like, “That’s dramatic, stop,” but it keeps coming back.\n\nIt scares me that I’m even thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my husband actually mentioned that. He said I’ve been moving around the house like I’m underwater, which was kind of a weird way to put it, but I knew what he meant.\n\nLike I’ll be going from the kitchen to the living room and it takes me forever, and I don’t even notice until he says something. Or he’ll ask me something simple—like where I put the mail, and the mail pile is its own whole disaster, honestly—and I’ll just… sit there for a second. The words are in my head, sort of, but they come out really slowly.\n\nSo, yeah, more slowed down than restless, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or blaming yourself more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep going back to this mortgage decision we made, and I just… I can’t stop thinking I was selfish and reckless.\n\nLike, my husband keeps reminding me that we both agreed to it, we talked it through together, and it wasn’t just me signing something in secret or whatever. But in my head it turns into, “You put the whole household at risk.” And then I’ll start thinking about interest rates, and our kitchen needs work too, which is a whole other thing because the cabinet door has been loose for months and I keep saying I’ll fix it—\n\nSorry. But yes. I’m blaming myself for it a lot more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or like you can't sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Not so much like I can’t sit still… it’s more the opposite, I think. My husband keeps saying I move around the house like I’m underwater, which sounds funny but also kind of awful because I know what he means.\n\nAnd if he asks me something simple, I’ll just… pause. Like the answer is there, but it takes forever to come out. Even my words feel slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. I mean, I’ll shift around sometimes, but that’s probably normal—our couch has this one cushion that kind of sinks in, and my husband keeps saying we should replace it—\n\nBut the thing he’s been pointing out is more that I’m slow. Like I’ll walk through the house and he says it’s like I’m moving underwater. And if he asks me something simple, I’ll just sit there for a bit before answering, because the words come out so slowly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. I don’t feel like I’m bouncing my leg or needing to get up all the time.\n\nIt’s more that my husband keeps noticing I’m slowed down. He says I move around the house like I’m underwater, which… I hate hearing, but it’s kind of true. And if he asks me something simple, I’ll just sit there for a while before I answer, like the words are stuck or coming out way too slowly.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've lost interest in recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Probably about two months? It kind of started after we took on the mortgage, which sounds silly because everyone has bills, but it felt like this big switch flipped.

Before that I was in this really overcharged push with the business stuff, like I had all these plans and couldn’t stop thinking about it, and then after the mortgage thing settled in, I just… dropped into this low stretch. And that’s when the things I usually enjoy started feeling like too much.

I used to be pretty good about making time for things—hiking on the weekends, yoga, messing around in my garden. But then I also feel like everyone worries about that.\n\nIt’s funny, my neighbor has this dog that barks at every delivery truck, and sometimes I’ll be sitting there hearing it and suddenly I’m thinking about how I need to order paper towels, and then I’m thinking about money, and then work, and then I kind of lose track of what I was doing. But I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just… my brain being annoying.\n\nSo, I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it as the main thing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been about two months? Around when we took on the mortgage. It’s funny, because everyone tells you buying a place is supposed to feel exciting, and I was doing this whole intense push around business ideas and numbers and plans, like I could just force everything to work out.\n\nAnd then after that, I don’t know… it sort of dropped out from under me. Since then it’s been this low stretch. So, yeah, roughly two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I had this whole thing where I’d notice different ones around the neighborhood—there was this one little finch that kept coming back near the fence—but now the camera’s just sitting in the closet. It all feels like more effort than it’s worth, which is sad because those were the things I used to actually make time for.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I don’t know if it’s “any pleasure at all,” exactly… like, I’m not sure how to measure that. But the things that usually make me feel like myself just don’t really land anymore.

So… yeah, that’s why I came in. I’m tired in a way that doesn’t feel normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There’s this one patch where the grass never grows right, and I start thinking I should fix it, then somehow I’m thinking about the whole house and work and everything, and it just feels like there isn’t really a good version of the future coming. So… yes, sad, or down, or whatever you’d call that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s changed. I used to be pretty good about making time for things—hiking on the weekends, yoga, messing around in my garden. I mean, I worry about normal things, I guess—work stuff, whether I forgot to send some report, whether the house is a mess. But then I also feel like everyone worries about that.\n\nIt’s funny, my neighbor has this dog that barks at every delivery truck, and sometimes I’ll be sitting there hearing it and suddenly I’m thinking about how I need to order paper towels, and then I’m thinking about money, and then work, and then I kind of lose track of what I was doing. But I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just… my brain being annoying.\n\nSo, I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed it as the main thing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been about two months? Around when we took on the mortgage. It’s funny, because everyone tells you buying a place is supposed to feel exciting, and I was doing this whole intense push around business ideas and numbers and plans, like I could just force everything to work out.\n\nAnd then after that, I don’t know… it sort of dropped out from under me. Since then it’s been this low stretch. So, yeah, roughly two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I keep thinking maybe if I just get myself out there I’ll enjoy it once I’m doing it, like with hiking or yoga or even the garden, but then I end up canceling or just… not going.\n\nI used to really look forward to taking bird photos too. I had this whole thing where I’d notice different ones around the neighborhood—there was this one little finch that kept coming back near the fence—but now the camera’s just sitting in the closet. I mean, my jeans feel… basically the same, I think. Not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping or noticing any changes in your sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t been keeping track of it, really. Sorry—that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed it enough to wonder about it, but I couldn’t give you a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve made some plan or anything. But late at night, when my husband’s asleep and the house is quiet—except the fridge makes this clicking sound sometimes, which drives me nuts—I’ll catch myself thinking maybe he’d be better off without me. And then I try to push it away, like, “That’s dramatic, stop,” but it keeps coming back.\n\nIt scares me that I’m even thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my husband actually mentioned that. The words are in my head, sort of, but they come out really slowly.\n\nSo, yeah, more slowed down than restless, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or blaming yourself more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep going back to this mortgage decision we made, and I just… I can’t stop thinking I was selfish and reckless.\n\nLike, my husband keeps reminding me that we both agreed to it, we talked it through together, and it wasn’t just me signing something in secret or whatever. It’s funny, because everyone tells you buying a place is supposed to feel exciting, and I was doing this whole intense push around business ideas and numbers and plans, like I could just force everything to work out.\n\nAnd then after that, I don’t know… it sort of dropped out from under me. Since then it’s been this low stretch. So, yeah, roughly two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I keep thinking maybe if I just get myself out there I’ll enjoy it once I’m doing it, like with hiking or yoga or even the garden, but then I end up canceling or just… not going.\n\nI used to really look forward to taking bird photos too. I had this whole thing where I’d notice different ones around the neighborhood—there was this one little finch that kept coming back near the fence—but now the camera’s just sitting in the closet. It all feels like more effort than it’s worth, which is sad because those were the things I used to actually make time for.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, my jeans feel… basically the same, I think. I haven’t really been weighing myself.\n\nWe have this scale in the bathroom, but it’s kind of wedged next to the laundry basket, and half the time there’s towels on it because my husband just puts things anywhere, which drives me crazy—sorry, that’s not really the question.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed any big weight change. Not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping or noticing any changes in your sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t been keeping track of it, really. I mean, I go to bed, and then sometimes I’m just sort of aware of the time, but I don’t know if that’s “trouble sleeping” or just normal stress stuff.\n\nOur bedroom is kind of annoying lately too because the curtains don’t block the streetlight well, and I keep meaning to order different ones, but then I get stuck comparing reviews for like an hour and don’t buy anything. Sorry—that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’ve noticed it enough to wonder about it, but I couldn’t give you a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really like saying that out loud.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve made some plan or anything. But late at night, when my husband’s asleep and the house is quiet—except the fridge makes this clicking sound sometimes, which drives me nuts—I’ll catch myself thinking maybe he’d be better off without me. And then I try to push it away, like, “That’s dramatic, stop,” but it keeps coming back.\n\nIt scares me that I’m even thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my husband actually mentioned that. He said I’ve been moving around the house like I’m underwater, which was kind of a weird way to put it, but I knew what he meant.\n\nLike I’ll be going from the kitchen to the living room and it takes me forever, and I don’t even notice until he says something. Or he’ll ask me something simple—like where I put the mail, and the mail pile is its own whole disaster, honestly—and I’ll just… sit there for a second. The words are in my head, sort of, but they come out really slowly.\n\nSo, yeah, more slowed down than restless, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or blaming yourself more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep going back to this mortgage decision we made, and I just… I can’t stop thinking I was selfish and reckless.\n\nLike, my husband keeps reminding me that we both agreed to it, we talked it through together, and it wasn’t just me signing something in secret or whatever. But in my head it turns into, “You put the whole household at risk.” And then I’ll start thinking about interest rates, and our kitchen needs work too, which is a whole other thing because the cabinet door has been loose for months and I keep saying I’ll fix it—\n\nSorry. But yes. I’m blaming myself for it a lot more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or like you can't sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Not so much like I can’t sit still… it’s more the opposite, I think. My husband keeps saying I move around the house like I’m underwater, which sounds funny but also kind of awful because I know what he means.\n\nAnd if he asks me something simple, I’ll just… pause. Like the answer is there, but it takes forever to come out. Even my words feel slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. I mean, I’ll shift around sometimes, but that’s probably normal—our couch has this one cushion that kind of sinks in, and my husband keeps saying we should replace it—\n\nBut the thing he’s been pointing out is more that I’m slow. Like I’ll walk through the house and he says it’s like I’m moving underwater. And if he asks me something simple, I’ll just sit there for a bit before answering, because the words come out so slowly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. I don’t feel like I’m bouncing my leg or needing to get up all the time.\n\nIt’s more that my husband keeps noticing I’m slowed down. He says I move around the house like I’m underwater, which… I hate hearing, but it’s kind of true. And if he asks me something simple, I’ll just sit there for a while before I answer, like the words are stuck or coming out way too slowly.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've lost interest in recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to really make time for things, like hiking or yoga, and I had this whole little routine with my garden—tomatoes never did great, but I kept trying anyway. And I used to take my camera out for bird photos, even just around the neighborhood.\n\nLately I keep canceling hikes or just not going to yoga, and the garden is kind of… sitting there. Lately I keep canceling or just not going, and the camera’s been sitting in the closet because I look at it and think… what’s the point, it’s too much effort. It doesn’t feel worth it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your low mood and loss of interest in activities are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think it is. I keep telling myself I’m still functioning, because I’m technically showing up, but I’ve fallen behind on client reports, and then I avoid opening the files because I know how behind I am.\n\nAnd at home it’s like… I do the basics, dishes if they’re really bad, laundry when we’re out of towels. I used to go outside to kind of reset myself, or meet friends for a walk, and now I keep dodging texts or saying I’m busy. Which is ridiculous, because I’m not really busy in a useful way—I’m just using all my energy to get through work and keep the house from totally falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to move around or pace when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really pacing, no… it’s almost the opposite, I guess. It’s usually worse in the morning, I think. I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling in my chest before I’ve even done anything wrong, if that makes sense.

Sometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table with coffee going cold, staring out at the yard, and my brain starts doing this thing where it’s like… there isn’t a good version of the future coming. And then I’ll notice the weeds by the fence or the bird feeder needs filling, and I’ll think about how I used to care about little stuff like that, and then I’m off thinking about ten other things. He said I’ve been moving around the house like I’m underwater, which was kind of a weird way to put it, but I knew what he meant.\n\nLike I’ll be going from the kitchen to the living room and it takes me forever, and I don’t even notice until he says something. Or he’ll ask me something simple—like where I put the mail, and the mail pile is its own whole disaster, honestly—and I’ll just… sit there for a second. The words are in my head, sort of, but they come out really slowly.\n\nSo, yeah, more slowed down than restless, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or blaming yourself more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep going back to this mortgage decision we made, and I just… I can’t stop thinking I was selfish and reckless.\n\nLike, my husband keeps reminding me that we both agreed to it, we talked it through together, and it wasn’t just me signing something in secret or whatever. But in my head it turns into, “You put the whole household at risk.” And then I’ll start thinking about interest rates, and our kitchen needs work too, which is a whole other thing because the cabinet door has been loose for months and I keep saying I’ll fix it—\n\nSorry. But yes. I’m blaming myself for it a lot more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or like you can't sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Not so much like I can’t sit still… it’s more the opposite, I think. My husband keeps saying I move around the house like I’m underwater, which sounds funny but also kind of awful because I know what he means.\n\nAnd if he asks me something simple, I’ll just… pause. Like the answer is there, but it takes forever to come out. Even my words feel slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. I mean, I’ll shift around sometimes, but that’s probably normal—our couch has this one cushion that kind of sinks in, and my husband keeps saying we should replace it—\n\nBut the thing he’s been pointing out is more that I’m slow. Like I’ll walk through the house and he says it’s like I’m moving underwater. And if he asks me something simple, I’ll just sit there for a bit before answering, because the words come out so slowly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or having trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. I don’t feel like I’m bouncing my leg or needing to get up all the time.\n\nIt’s more that my husband keeps noticing I’m slowed down. He says I move around the house like I’m underwater, which… I hate hearing, but it’s kind of true. And if he asks me something simple, I’ll just sit there for a while before I answer, like the words are stuck or coming out way too slowly.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you've lost interest in recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to really make time for things, like hiking or yoga, and I had this whole little routine with my garden—tomatoes never did great, but I kept trying anyway. And I used to take my camera out for bird photos, even just around the neighborhood.\n\nLately I keep canceling hikes or just not going to yoga, and the garden is kind of… sitting there. My camera’s been in the closet for weeks, maybe longer. It all just feels like too much effort, or like it won’t be worth it once I get there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Probably about two months? It kind of started after we took on the mortgage, which sounds silly because everyone has bills, but it felt like this big switch flipped.\n\nBefore that I was in this really overcharged push with the business stuff, like I had all these plans and couldn’t stop thinking about it, and then after the mortgage thing settled in, I just… dropped into this low stretch. And that’s when the things I usually enjoy started feeling like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any pleasure at all, even from things that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “any pleasure at all,” exactly… like, I’m not sure how to measure that. But the things that usually make me feel like myself just don’t really land anymore.\n\nI used to make time for hiking, yoga, messing with the garden, taking my camera out for bird photos—there’s this one little patch near our street where I used to see finches all the time, and I’d get weirdly excited about it. Lately I keep canceling or just not going, and the camera’s been sitting in the closet because I look at it and think… what’s the point, it’s too much effort. It doesn’t feel worth it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your low mood and loss of interest in activities are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think it is. I keep telling myself I’m still functioning, because I’m technically showing up, but I’ve fallen behind on client reports, and then I avoid opening the files because I know how behind I am.\n\nAnd at home it’s like… I do the basics, dishes if they’re really bad, laundry when we’re out of towels. I used to go outside to kind of reset myself, or meet friends for a walk, and now I keep dodging texts or saying I’m busy. Which is ridiculous, because I’m not really busy in a useful way—I’m just using all my energy to get through work and keep the house from totally falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to move around or pace when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: Not really pacing, no… it’s almost the opposite, I guess. My husband actually said the other day that I move around the house like I’m underwater, which bothered me more than I wanted to admit.\n\nLike, he’ll ask me something simple—where I put the mail, or whether I want tea—and I hear him, but it takes me a while to answer. The words just come out really slowly. And then I feel embarrassed because it’s not a hard question, you know? It’s like everything has this delay on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your low mood is worse in the morning or if it stays the same throughout the day?\n\nPatient: It’s usually worse in the morning, I think. I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling in my chest before I’ve even done anything wrong, if that makes sense.\n\nSometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table with coffee going cold, staring out at the yard, and my brain starts doing this thing where it’s like… there isn’t a good version of the future coming. And then I’ll notice the weeds by the fence or the bird feeder needs filling, and I’ll think about how I used to care about little stuff like that, and then I’m off thinking about ten other things. But yeah—the mornings are the worst."
